# Daemon core test audit

Scope: 70 assigned test files, 703 baseline named declarations (parameterized declarations are one audit row). Counts: CONSOLIDATE 6, DELETE 10, KEEP 671, OPTIMIZE 5, REWRITE 11.

Test LOC: baseline 17,604; final 17,429; net -175 (127 added, 302 removed). Named declarations fall by 13: ten DELETE cases and three removed consolidation participants. The six CONSOLIDATE rows include three surviving owners. No fixtures, snapshots, dependencies or configuration are removed within this ownership scope; root separately removes the obsolete assertion/timer preload infrastructure.

Rows reference baseline source locations and exact declaration names. A KEEP is a case decision after inspecting its assertions/actions with the production owner; each file's shared rationale supplies contract source, stable seam, independent oracle, refactor resistance, duplicate boundary and callers. The case row states its specific failure and an assertion excerpt. Literal fixtures/original arranged input, never the result of the implementation under test, supply expected values. Controlled dependencies isolate filesystem/process/provider timing; they do not manufacture asserted service decisions.

No daemon or live service was started. All actual external processes use scratch appdirs, ephemeral listeners or dedicated tmux sockets. Root coordinates baseline/full validation; this report does not claim unexecuted focused checks.

## Changes and infrastructure

- Remove private safeCutIndex/Gate/lineDiffCounts/formatEnvValue exports after repository-wide caller search; production bodies remain in use internally. Remove obsolete imports, private-map coverage and redundant HTTP fixtures. No fixture/snapshot/dependency is orphaned by these deletions.
- Keep atomic create-only disk-full/racing-file fallback tests: they verify actual file contents/existence after explicit I/O failures, rather than mocked assertions about call shape. The limited fault seam remains necessary to exercise filesystems without hard links safely.
- NODE_OPTIONS expectations no longer call the production formatter. Private preferred quoting and size-dependent diff algorithm expectations disappear. Public comparison/merge outputs retain the relevant value and line-count checks.
- WebSocket cleanup, fake-app exit-file completion and controlled queue deadlines target measured teardown/wait bottlenecks. Real Chromium, Xvnc/Openbox, PulseAudio and 16-bit X11 wrap coverage remains because it owns actual integration/protocol failures.

## Performance and validation

Baseline measurements below come from root's full `pnpm test` run, Node v24.22.1, the package default file concurrency of 4 and package-serial execution. Case times include node:test hooks; identical title collisions cannot provide unambiguous file timing. They are per-case evidence, not summed suite wall time. Root's final report records comparable whole-suite timing and final gate commands.

Measured baseline bottlenecks: VNC pre-connect ordering case 30,067.7 ms (unacknowledged raw-client close teardown); VM signal owner case 5,049.5 ms (fake apps never write exit files); browser queue case includes two 500 ms real waits. Exact after measurements and executed validation are appended when available. No CI speedup is extrapolated.

Retained costly integration: actual large PNG→JPEG/capped bytes, isolated fresh browser profiles and SSRF, frame measurements, actual desktop process group teardown, real audio and 65,536 X11 requests across sequence wrap. Each catches failures controlled collaborators cannot reproduce without duplicating implementation. Their environment-dependent timing remains a maintenance cost.

## Case ledger

### apps/daemon/src/agent-conversations.test.ts

1. Independent source: README history/resume and packages/api/src/index.ts AgentConversationSummary; CLI transcript filesystem formats.
2. Observable seam: listAgentConversations over real scratch transcript directories. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Unique transcript discovery/home attribution: provider runtime replay tests start after discovery and do not exercise these directories.
6. Non-test callers: index.ts conversation routes; resume consumes account attribution. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

### apps/daemon/src/saved-prompts-routes.test.ts

1. Independent source: packages/api/src/saved-prompts.ts HTTP contracts; README project/workspace deletion and server-stamped principal security.
2. Observable seam: createServer HTTP injection with real SavedPromptsService and scratch files. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Owns integrated HTTP adoption, cascades after successful filesystem deletion and principal enrichment. Non-protective readOnly probe is removed; actual no-write contract remains in service suite.
6. Non-test callers: index.ts saved-prompt routes/cascades. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.

### apps/daemon/src/search.test.ts

1. Independent source: README file search/gitignore/sandbox behavior; packages/api FsSearch/FsFileList response offsets, caps and refusal contracts; packages/config glob grammar.
2. Observable seam: searchProjectFiles/listProjectFiles against actual scratch files, Git and both real-ripgrep/fallback-node discovery paths. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Different engines own distinct code/protocol paths: retaining shared behavioral examples detects backend disagreement rather than duplicate implementation. Explicit Unicode offsets, symlink/no-follow, ignored files, caps, cancellation and contextual refusal cases are not covered by the pure glob compiler.
6. Non-test callers: index.ts file search and listing routes; workflow file readers. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

### apps/daemon/src/secret-files/files.test.ts

1. Independent source: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-secret-files-plugin-design.md §§2–3; dotenv, Git literal pathspecs and filesystem no-follow/O_EXCL/atomic-durability guarantees.
2. Observable seam: readCloneFile/writeCloneFile/scanCandidates/trackedPaths/compareContents/mergeEnvContents; actual scratch files/Git. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Only low filesystem safety and parsing owner; in-memory service fakes cannot catch symlink following, modes or literal git paths. Private quoting/large-diff algorithm expectations removed; public compare and merge retain meaningful results.
6. Non-test callers: secret-files/service.ts and boxes/remote/fs/secret-files.ts. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

### apps/daemon/src/secret-files/routes.test.ts

1. Independent source: Secret Files design §9 and packages/api secretFilesRoutes, codes, server principal/privacy contract.
2. Observable seam: Fastify route injection and actual createServer remote/local registration. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Owns request validation/envelope/auth/principal boundary; service tests own sync logic, while real service route scenarios ensure host-only values never serialize.
6. Non-test callers: index.ts Secret Files route mounting. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. ### apps/daemon/src/sessions-activity.test.ts

1. Independent source: README attention signalling; session activity and lifecycle ordering consumed by clients.
2. Observable seam: LocalSessionManager real exiting PTY with lifecycle/activity output. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Uniquely verifies backend exit wiring/order; ActivityTracker unit tests cannot detect manager forgetting or prematurely broadcasting exit attention.
6. Non-test callers: sessions.ts PTY process lifecycle. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

| Baseline path:line | Exact test declaration | Disposition | Specific failure / oracle evidence / surviving owner | Baseline ms |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| apps/daemon/src/sessions-activity.test.ts:12 | LocalSessionManager raises finished attention when the command exits | KEEP | Detects: LocalSessionManager raises finished attention when the command exits. Oracle/seam evidence: assert.equal(activity.state, "idle"); assert.equal(typeof activity.needsAttentionAt, "string") | 67.044 |

### apps/daemon/src/system-status.test.ts

1. Independent source: packages/api SystemResources/Processes/Details contracts; Linux proc(5) formats; README system security and own-user/process protection.
2. Observable seam: public parsers plus SystemStatusService on actual isolated processes and /proc. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Raw fixtures own numeric/protocol parsing; real process cases uniquely own process identity/protected ancestor/UID/signal behavior and orphan attribution. Retained parser tests do not spawn expensive hosts.
6. Non-test callers: index.ts system status/process API. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.

### apps/daemon/src/terminal-history.test.ts

1. Independent source: packages/api terminal-stream.ts replay bytes and README reconnect scrollback; ECMA-48 query suppression/Unicode preservation.
2. Observable seam: TerminalOutputRing and sanitizeTerminalReplayChunk outputs. Each row names a recognizable incorrect result/state/refusal/lost-or-leaked byte; performance cases additionally detect bounded-work regressions.
3. Independent oracle: explicit fixture bytes, arranged file contents, expected error/status/state values or a hand-controlled time/side-effect boundary. Fixture-derived identifiers/timestamps are inputs, rather than a second call to the behavior under test.
4. Refactor resistance: retained assertions observe returned/public persisted data, protocol frames, actual child environment/process completion or externally visible filesystem effects. Internal algorithms, container layouts and preferred formatting may change without changing these observations.
5. Unique protection: Ring tests own externally replayed content/cap/boundary effects; byte filter tables own terminal query classes. Removed safeCutIndex helper tests duplicated ring results and pinned a private integer strategy.
6. Non-test callers: sessions.ts and boxes/remote/exec/terminals.ts. Deletion risk: preserve privacy/data-loss/process-ownership/storage contracts; remove only the specific failed-bar scenarios described below. Validation: root `pnpm test` plus affected-package `pnpm check:changed`; focused paths appear in the validation addendum.
